### 2026-hsd-reference-file-updated-12-17-2025.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 23
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted provider meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not supplement a primary care physician’s care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
036 - Clinical Psychology | A clinical psychologist is a medical professional with a doctoral degree in psychology and is licensed or certified by the State in which the service is performed at the independent practice level of psychology to furnish diagnostic, assessment, preventive, and therapeutic services directly to individuals. 
```

### 2025-hsd-reference-file-updated-12032024.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 23
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted provider meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not supplement a primary care physician’s care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
036 - Clinical Psychology | In April 2023, CMS published a final rule which added a Clinical Psychology provider specialty type to the network adequacy standards. A clinical psychologist is a medical professional with a doctoral degree in psychology and is licensed or certified by the State in which the service is performed at the independent practice level of psychology to furnish diagnostic, assessment, preventive, and therapeutic services directly to individuals. 
```

### 2024-hsd-reference-file-updated-10182023.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 20
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted provider meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not supplement a primary care physician’s care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
036 - Clinical Psychology | In April 2023, CMS published a final rule which added a Clinical Psychology provider specialty type to the network adequacy standards. A clinical psychologist is a medical professional with a doctoral degree in psychology and is licensed or certified by the State in which the service is performed at the independent practice level of psychology to furnish diagnostic, assessment, preventive, and therapeutic services directly to individuals. 
```

### 2023-hsd-reference-file-updated-09082022.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 18
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted provider meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not supplement a primary care physician’s care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
040 - Acute Inpatient Hospitals | Organizations must submit at least one contracted acute inpatient hospital. Organizations may need to submit more than one acute inpatient hospital in order to satisfy the time/distance criteria. There are Minimum Number criteria for the acute inpatient hospital specialty. Organizations must demonstrate that their contracted acute inpatient hospitals have at least the minimum number of Medicare-certified hospital beds. The minimum number of Medicare-certified acute inpatient hospital beds, by county, can be found on the “Minimum Facility #s” tab of the HSD Reference File. 
```

### 2022-hsd-reference-file-updated-08252021.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 18
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted provider meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not supplement a primary care physician’s care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
040 - Acute Inpatient Hospitals | Organizations must submit at least one contracted acute inpatient hospital. Organizations may need to submit more than one acute inpatient hospital in order to satisfy the time/distance criteria. There are Minimum Number criteria for the acute inpatient hospital specialty. Organizations must demonstrate that their contracted acute inpatient hospitals have at least the minimum number of Medicare-certified hospital beds. The minimum number of Medicare-certified acute inpatient hospital beds, by county, can be found on the “Minimum Facility #s” tab of the HSD Reference File. 
```

### 2021-hsd-reference-file-updated-08132020.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 18
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted provider meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician’s care,  and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
040 - Acute Inpatient Hospitals | Organizations must submit at least one contracted acute inpatient hospital. Organizations may need to submit more than one acute inpatient hospital in order to satisfy the time/distance criteria. There are Minimum Number criteria for the acute inpatient hospital specialty. Organizations must demonstrate that their contracted acute inpatient hospitals have at least the minimum number of Medicare-certified hospital beds. The minimum number of Medicare-certified acute inpatient hospital beds, by county, can be found on the “Minimum Facility #s” tab of the HSD Reference File. 
```

### 2021-hsd-reference-file-updated-08132020.xlsx / Criteria Changes

- rows: 6850
```
This tab includes county*specialty instances where criteria have changed between 2020 and 2021. Definitions: 1) Return to Base – Mapping shows that it is possible to pass the standard base distance (see Master tab), so the criterion is no longer customized. 2) Resetting More Stringent – Mapping shows that it is possible to pass at a smaller time and/or distance than the 2020 customized time and distance so the time and/or distance values have decreased from 2020, but are still larger than the standard base distance. 3) Customization - Time and/or Distance values have increased since 2020 (this indicator takes precedence over County Designation Change). 4) County Designation Change - Time and/or Distance values have changed since 2020 due to a change in the county designation. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
```

### 2020-hsd-reference-file-updated-01142020.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 17
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted provider meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician’s care,  and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
040 - Acute Inpatient Hospitals | Organizations must submit at least one contracted acute inpatient hospital. Organizations may need to submit more than one acute inpatient hospital in order to satisfy the time/distance criteria. There are Minimum Number criteria for the acute inpatient hospital specialty. Organizations must demonstrate that their contracted acute inpatient hospitals have at least the minimum number of Medicare-certified hospital beds. The minimum number of Medicare-certified acute inpatient hospital beds, by county, can be found on the “Minimum Facility #s” tab of the HSD Reference File. 
```

### 2019-hsd-reference-file-updated-08012018.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 17
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted providers meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician’s care,  and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
040 - Acute Inpatient Hospitals | Organizations must submit at least one contracted acute inpatient hospital. Organizations may need to submit more than one acute inpatient hospital in order to satisfy the time/distance criteria. There are Minimum Number criteria for the acute inpatient hospital specialty. Organizations must demonstrate that their contracted acute inpatient hospitals have at least the minimum number of Medicare-certified hospital beds. The minimum number of Medicare-certified acute inpatient hospital beds, by county, can be found on the “Minimum Facility #s” tab of the HSD Reference File. 
```

### 2018-hsd-reference-file-updated-12222016.xlsx / Notes

- rows: 26
```
This tab includes instructions for several specialty types, including definitions and what is appropriate for inclusion on the HSD table submissions. | 
SPECIALTY TYPE | NOTES
001 - General Practice | See note for S03 
002 - Family Practice | See note for S03
003 - Internal Medicine | See note for S03
004 - Geriatrics | See note for S03, and: Submissions appropriate for this specialty code are internal medicine, family practice, and general practice physicians who have a special knowledge of the aging process and special skills and who focus upon the diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of illnesses pertinent to the elderly. 
005 - Primary Care - 
           Physician Assistants | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted providers meets the applicable state requirements governing the qualifications for assistants to primary care physicians and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician's care, and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area. 
006 - Primary Care - 
           Nurse Practitioners | See note for S03, and: The purpose of this specialty’s inclusion, and its inclusion in the Provider HSD Table, is to inform CMS of the rare contracting with non-MD primary care providers in underserved counties to serve as the major source of primary care for enrollees. Organizations may include submissions under this specialty code only if the contracted registered professional nurse is currently licensed in the state, meets the state’s requirements governing the qualifications of nurse practitioners, and is fully credentialed by the organization as a provider of primary care services. In addition, in accordance with state law, the providers listed under this specialty code must function as the primary care source for the enrollee, not  supplement a primary care physician’s care,  and be practicing in or rendering services to enrollees residing in a state and/or federally designated physician manpower shortage area.
S03 - Primary Care Providers | The following six specialty types are reported separately on the Provider HSD Table: General Practice (001), Family Practice (002), Internal Medicine (003), Geriatrics (004), Primary Care – Physician Assistants (005), Primary Care – Nurse Practitioners (006).

Organizations submit contracted providers using the appropriate individual specialty codes (001 – 006). CMS sums these providers, maps them as a single group, and evaluates the results of those submissions whose office locations are within the prescribed time and distance standards for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers. These six individual specialty types are also summed and evaluated as a single group against the Minimum Number of Primary Care Providers criteria (note that in order to apply toward the minimum number, a provider must be within the prescribed time and distance standards). There are network adequacy criteria for the specialty type: Primary Care Providers, and not for the individual specialties. The criteria and the results of the Automated Criteria Check (ACC) are reported under the specialty type: S03.
026 - Physiatry, Rehabilitative Medicine | A physiatrist, or physical medicine and rehabilitation specialist, is a medical doctor who is trained in the diagnosis and treatment of patients with physical, functionally limiting, and/or painful conditions. These specialists focus upon the maximal restoration of physical function through comprehensive rehabilitation and pain management therapies. Physical Therapists are NOT Physiatry/Rehabilitative Medicine physicians and are not to be included on the Provider HSD Tables under this specialty type.
035 - Cardiothoracic Surgery  | Cardiothoracic surgeons provide operative, perioperative, and surgical critical care to patients with acquired and congenital pathologic conditions within the chest. This includes the surgical repair of congenital and acquired conditions of the heart, including the pericardium, coronary arteries, valves, great vessels and myocardium. Cardiologists, including interventional cardiologists, are not cardiothoracic surgeons, and may not be included under this specialty type.
040 - Acute Inpatient Hospitals | Organizations must submit at least one contracted acute inpatient hospital. Organizations may need to submit more than one acute inpatient hospital in order to satisfy the time/distance criteria. There are Minimum Number criteria for the acute inpatient hospital specialty. Organizations must demonstrate that their contracted acute inpatient hospitals have at least the minimum number of Medicare-certified hospital beds. The minimum number of Medicare-certified acute inpatient hospital beds, by county, can be found on the “Minimum Facility #s” tab of the HSD Reference File. 
```
